But, I figured I should come in anyway since they told me to.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I am. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even just a pound, or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I just don't want to end up slower moving around the cars at the garage, or up and down the court. So, yeah, I definitely don't want to put weight on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: I think it's fine. I mean, I know people are worried about it, but I figure they’re just making way too much of it. I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I play ball on the weekends. If it was really a problem, I wouldn't be able to do all that, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin or just right when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I think I look just right. When I look in the mirror, I just see a guy who's in shape. I don't really see what that doctor at the physical was so worked up about. I just look lean, you know? I definitely don't think I look too thin. So, yeah, to me, I look fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever check your weight or body shape in the mirror more than once a day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get dressed in the morning and maybe glance in the bathroom mirror while I'm shaving, but that's about it. I don't stand around staring at myself or checking the scale all day. I just get ready and head to the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is much larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? I definitely don't sit down and eat a ton of food all at once. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, never. I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, if I eat something, it stays down. I've never tried to make myself throw up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your weight or body shape has a significant impact on your self-worth?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I base how I feel about myself on my work, mostly. If I do a good job at the shop and get a car running right, that's what makes me feel good. I don't really tie my worth as a person to my weight or what my body looks like. It's just not something I think about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you need to achieve to feel good about yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have some specific number on the scale I'm trying to hit. I just... I don't really think about it like that. There's no perfect shape or exact weight I'm aiming for just to feel good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends or family see you as being underweight or having a weight problem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re just making way too much of it, honestly. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I still play ball on the weekends. If there was really a problem, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, you know? So I just think they're overreacting.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

But, I figured I should come in anyway since they told me to.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I am. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even just a pound, or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I just get ready and head to the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is much larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, never. I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, if I eat something, it stays down. I've never tried to make myself throw up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your weight or body shape has a significant impact on your self-worth?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I base how I feel about myself on my work, mostly. If I do a good job at the shop and get a car running right, that's what makes me feel good. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, it was my work physical that got me here. The doctor there said my weight is too low and wanted me to get checked out. I don't really see the problem, though. I just feel lighter on my feet when I play basketball, and I don't feel as weighed down when I'm working at the garage. But, I figured I should come in anyway since they told me to.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I am. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even just a pound, or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I just don't want to end up slower moving around the cars at the garage, or up and down the court. So, yeah, I definitely don't want to put weight on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: I think it's fine. I mean, I know people are worried about it, but I figure they’re just making way too much of it. I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I play ball on the weekends. If it was really a problem, I wouldn't be able to do all that, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin or just right when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I think I look just right. When I look in the mirror, I just see a guy who's in shape. I don't really see what that doctor at the physical was so worked up about. I just look lean, you know? I definitely don't think I look too thin. So, yeah, to me, I look fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever check your weight or body shape in the mirror more than once a day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get dressed in the morning and maybe glance in the bathroom mirror while I'm shaving, but that's about it. I don't stand around staring at myself or checking the scale all day. I just get ready and head to the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is much larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? I definitely don't sit down and eat a ton of food all at once. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, never. I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, if I eat something, it stays down. I've never tried to make myself throw up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your weight or body shape has a significant impact on your self-worth?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I base how I feel about myself on my work, mostly. If I do a good job at the shop and get a car running right, that's what makes me feel good. I don't really tie my worth as a person to my weight or what my body looks like. It's just not something I think about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you need to achieve to feel good about yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have some specific number on the scale I'm trying to hit. I just... I don't really think about it like that. There's no perfect shape or exact weight I'm aiming for just to feel good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends or family see you as being underweight or having a weight problem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re just making way too much of it, honestly. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I still play ball on the weekends. If there was really a problem, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, you know? So I just think they're overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends and family are correct that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're right. I mean, I know I'm on the leaner side, but I don't think I'm underweight like they say. I feel fine, I have the energy to do my job and get through the day. I think they just see what they want to see, or maybe they're comparing me to guys who are bigger. But for me, my weight is fine. I'm not underweight.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your body shape or weight, do you think it's different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess it must be different, since they're all saying I'm too thin and I just don't see it that way. I look in the mirror and I just see myself—a regular guy who works hard. I mean, I know I'm on the leaner side, but I don't think I'm underweight like they say. I feel fine, I have the energy to do my job and get through the day. I think they just see what they want to see, or maybe they're comparing me to guys who are bigger. But for me, my weight is fine. I'm not underweight.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your body shape or weight, do you think it's different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess it must be different, since they're all saying I'm too thin and I just don't see it that way. I look in the mirror and I just see myself—a regular guy who works hard. I don't see whatever it is they're seeing that has them so worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape or weight is normal for your age and height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I'm 44, I'm on my feet all day at the shop... it makes sense that I'm not carrying around a bunch of extra weight. For my height and everything, I think I look perfectly normal. I'm just built a little leaner, that's all.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight, do you believe you are underweight, even if just slightly?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know people are worried about me, but... I just figure they’re making way too much of it. If I was actually underweight, even a little bit, I wouldn't be able to keep up like I do. I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing around the house, and I even play ball on the weekends. So to me, that means I'm doing just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a difference between how you perceive your body shape and how it actually is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think there is. I mean, when I look in the mirror, I'm just seeing what's actually there. I know my family thinks I'm looking at it wrong, but... to me, my eyes work fine. What I see is exactly how I am. I don't feel like my mind is playing tricks on me or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But, I figured I should come in anyway since they told me to.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I am. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even just a pound, or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I just don't want to end up slower moving around the cars at the garage, or up and down the court. I mean, I know people are worried about it, but I figure they’re just making way too much of it. I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I play ball on the weekends. When I look in the mirror, I just see a guy who's in shape. I don't really see what that doctor at the physical was so worked up about. I just look lean, you know? I definitely don't think I look too thin. So, yeah, to me, I look fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever check your weight or body shape in the mirror more than once a day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get dressed in the morning and maybe glance in the bathroom mirror while I'm shaving, but that's about it. I don't stand around staring at myself or checking the scale all day. I just get ready and head to the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is much larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? I definitely don't sit down and eat a ton of food all at once. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, never. I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, if I eat something, it stays down. I've never tried to make myself throw up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your weight or body shape has a significant impact on your self-worth?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I base how I feel about myself on my work, mostly. If I do a good job at the shop and get a car running right, that's what makes me feel good. I don't really tie my worth as a person to my weight or what my body looks like. I just get ready and head to the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is much larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? I definitely don't sit down and eat a ton of food all at once. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, never. I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, if I eat something, it stays down. I've never tried to make myself throw up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your weight or body shape has a significant impact on your self-worth?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I base how I feel about myself on my work, mostly. If I do a good job at the shop and get a car running right, that's what makes me feel good. I don't really tie my worth as a person to my weight or what my body looks like. It's just not something I think about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you need to achieve to feel good about yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even just a pound, or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I mean, I know people are worried about it, but I figure they’re just making way too much of it. I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I play ball on the weekends. If it was really a problem, I wouldn't be able to do all that, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin or just right when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I think I look just right. When I look in the mirror, I just see a guy who's in shape. I don't really see what that doctor at the physical was so worked up about. I just look lean, you know? I definitely don't think I look too thin. So, yeah, to me, I look fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever check your weight or body shape in the mirror more than once a day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get dressed in the morning and maybe glance in the bathroom mirror while I'm shaving, but that's about it. I don't stand around staring at myself or checking the scale all day. I just get ready and head to the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is much larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? I definitely don't sit down and eat a ton of food all at once. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, never. I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, if I eat something, it stays down. I've never tried to make myself throw up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your weight or body shape has a significant impact on your self-worth?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I base how I feel about myself on my work, mostly. If I do a good job at the shop and get a car running right, that's what makes me feel good. I don't really tie my worth as a person to my weight or what my body looks like. It's just not something I think about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you need to achieve to feel good about yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have some specific number on the scale I'm trying to hit. I just... I don't really think about it like that. There's no perfect shape or exact weight I'm aiming for just to feel good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends or family see you as being underweight or having a weight problem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re just making way too much of it, honestly. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I still play ball on the weekends. If there was really a problem, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, you know? So I just think they're overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends and family are correct that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're right. I mean, I know I'm on the leaner side, but I don't think I'm underweight like they say. I feel fine, I have the energy to do my job and get through the day. I think they just see what they want to see, or maybe they're comparing me to guys who are bigger. But for me, my weight is fine. I'm not underweight.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your body shape or weight, do you think it's different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess it must be different, since they're all saying I'm too thin and I just don't see it that way. I look in the mirror and I just see myself—a regular guy who works hard. I don't see whatever it is they're seeing that has them so worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape or weight is normal for your age and height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I'm 44, I'm on my feet all day at the shop... it makes sense that I'm not carrying around a bunch of extra weight. For my height and everything, I think I look perfectly normal. I'm just built a little leaner, that's all.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight, do you believe you are underweight, even if just slightly?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know people are worried about me, but... I just figure they’re making way too much of it. If I was actually underweight, even a little bit, I wouldn't be able to keep up like I do. I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing around the house, and I even play ball on the weekends. So to me, that means I'm doing just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a difference between how you perceive your body shape and how it actually is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think there is. I mean, when I look in the mirror, I'm just seeing what's actually there. I know my family thinks I'm looking at it wrong, but... to me, my eyes work fine. What I see is exactly how I am. I don't feel like my mind is playing tricks on me or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look better if you gained a little weight?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't. I think if I gained weight, I'd just feel... heavy, you know? Like it would slow me down. I like being lean. It works for me. I don't see any reason why putting on weight would make me look better.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight affect what you choose to eat or drink on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does. Most days, I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house, it's just easier. I feel better when I stick to my own simple rules about what I eat. It keeps me from worrying about putting on any of that weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the types or amounts of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, I definitely limit the amounts. That's why I stick to just the coffee in the morning and only eat a small sandwich or half of whatever is around later on. It's just my own set of rules. With the shop always running late and the house being so busy, keeping the amounts small and simple like that... it just makes me feel better and makes sure I don't put on any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you think they will make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really certain foods. It's more about the amount, like I said. I just eat less of whatever's there. I don't really have a list of things I won't touch, I just keep the portions small.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just not something I think about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape you need to achieve to feel good about yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have some specific number on the scale I'm trying to hit. I just... I don't really think about it like that. There's no perfect shape or exact weight I'm aiming for just to feel good about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends or family see you as being underweight or having a weight problem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re just making way too much of it, honestly. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of what needs doing around the house, and I still play ball on the weekends. If there was really a problem, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, you know? So I just think they're overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends and family are correct that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're right. I mean, I know I'm on the leaner side, but I don't think I'm underweight like they say. I feel fine, I have the energy to do my job and get through the day. I think they just see what they want to see, or maybe they're comparing me to guys who are bigger. But for me, my weight is fine. I'm not underweight.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your body shape or weight, do you think it's different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess it must be different, since they're all saying I'm too thin and I just don't see it that way. I look in the mirror and I just see myself—a regular guy who works hard. I don't see whatever it is they're seeing that has them so worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape or weight is normal for your age and height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I'm 44, I'm on my feet all day at the shop... it makes sense that I'm not carrying around a bunch of extra weight. For my height and everything, I think I look perfectly normal. I'm just built a little leaner, that's all.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight, do you believe you are underweight, even if just slightly?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I know people are worried about me, but... I just figure they’re making way too much of it. If I was actually underweight, even a little bit, I wouldn't be able to keep up like I do. I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing around the house, and I even play ball on the weekends. So to me, that means I'm doing just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a difference between how you perceive your body shape and how it actually is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think there is. I mean, when I look in the mirror, I'm just seeing what's actually there. I know my family thinks I'm looking at it wrong, but... to me, my eyes work fine. What I see is exactly how I am. I don't feel like my mind is playing tricks on me or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look better if you gained a little weight?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't. I think if I gained weight, I'd just feel... heavy, you know? Like it would slow me down. I like being lean. It works for me. I don't see any reason why putting on weight would make me look better.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight affect what you choose to eat or drink on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does. Most days, I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house, it's just easier. I feel better when I stick to my own simple rules about what I eat. It keeps me from worrying about putting on any of that weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the types or amounts of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, I definitely limit the amounts. That's why I stick to just the coffee in the morning and only eat a small sandwich or half of whatever is around later on. It's just my own set of rules. With the shop always running late and the house being so busy, keeping the amounts small and simple like that... it just makes me feel better and makes sure I don't put on any weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating certain foods because you think they will make you gain weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really certain foods. It's more about the amount, like I said. I just eat less of whatever's there. I don't really have a list of things I won't touch, I just keep the portions small.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to eat more if you didn't worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, I've been doing it this way for a while now, just keeping things small and simple. It's hard to say if I'd eat more if I wasn't worried about the weight. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. It's just how I eat now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
